Consequence for doc maintenance — after ANY betting re-run, `AdaDopamine.md` §3.1.1 / §3.1.2 tables must be updated to the new values; a small delta is the expected behaviour, not a regression to investigate. Adding a `--seed` is a small change (it would not disturb existing callers if defaulted to `None`) and is the obvious next step if betting goes into a paper, but as of 2026-07-29 it is **unresolved** — do not assume a stored betting number can be regenerated.
  - **Llama GPQA re-run RESULT (`static_0729`, 2026-07-29): the paired statistics are now recomputable, and the dissociation holds across the whole sweep.** mean_bet 5.20→7.78 at +4 (+2.58/question paired, Wilcoxon p_adj=2e−48), **accuracy McNemar Holm p_adj = 1.00 in ALL 8 cells**. Resampling noise is small (every cell's mean_bet within ±0.2 of `static_0616`), so the trends replicate exactly — cite the re-run, but do not treat the small deltas as a finding. Two structural facts survive: **the negative arm is NOT monotone** (−2/−4 strongly significant, −6 attenuates, −8 is n.s. at p_adj=0.066), so Llama's interpretable under-wanting effect sits at MODERATE doses only; and **`acc_explicit_pct` tracks micro to within 0.5–0.8pp at every α** (commit_rate 96–98%) — unlike Qwen's +8, Llama never breaks format, so both accuracy readings agree.
  - **Statistics convention (load-bearing — mixing these silently corrupts a row):** bet-fraction rates use **all-sample denominators** (every row sums to 100.0%), `mean_bet` uses **valid replies only**. Same questions across α ⇒ **paired tests**: McNemar for accuracy, Wilcoxon for bets — not χ²/MWU. On MMLU (n=14,042) report **Cliff's δ**, never bare p — at that n every real effect has an astronomically small p (Llama +4 Wilcoxon underflows to 0).
  - **MMLU RESULT (frozen 2026-07-29): dissociation holds on both models** — bets move, accuracy does not (McNemar p_adj: Llama 0.241/0.890, Qwen 0.336 both cells; invalid 0.0 everywhere). Llama +4 mean_bet 4.42→7.49 (Δ=+3.06/question, **δ=+0.585 — the largest effect in the whole betting suite**, 62.9% of questions changed their bet); Qwen +4 5.10→5.57 (Δ=+0.46, δ=+0.093, 10.3% changed).
  - **⚠ Do NOT read that gap as "Qwen's wanting effect is ~5× weaker" — compare bet DISTRIBUTIONS first.** Qwen's MMLU baseline is nearly degenerate: **97.9% of questions bet 5** (bet0/bet2 both 0%), so α has almost no headroom and mean_bet can barely move — a baseline ceiling artifact, not a weak intervention. The SAME model on GPQA, where its baseline is dispersed, moves 5.57→7.11 (Δ=+1.53). Llama's MMLU baseline spans bet2/5/10 (26.8/68.3/4.7%), which is why α has room. **`% of questions whose bet changed` is the headroom-robust readout; `mean_bet` alone is not comparable across models/tasks with different baseline dispersion.**
  - **Parser + schema (2026-07-28).** `BET_LEADING_RE = r"^\s*[:：]?\s*(\d+)"` — the optional leading colon recovers Qwen's `': 5\nAnswer: A'`. `parse_output` now returns `(bet, answer, answer_src)` with `answer_src ∈ {explicit, fallback, ""}`, and `summarise()` emits `acc_explicit_pct` / `n_explicit_answer` / `ans_fallback_rate` so accuracy can be read on in-format replies only (guards against fallback-recovered answers inflating a degraded cell). Rows carry `sample_idx` + `raw` (the latter under `--save_all_raw`). **§3.1 IS NOW FROZEN (2026-07-29) — all four cells re-run, do not re-run again.** Authoritative dirs: `llama3/gpqa/bet/static_0729`, `llama3/mmlu/bet/static0729`, `qwen2.5/bet/gpqa`, `qwen2.5/bet/mmlu` (note the Llama MMLU dir has NO underscore before the date). The re-runs closed a schema gap: the pre-2026-07-29 CSVs had **no `sample_idx`**, and §3.1's headline McNemar/Wilcoxon are PAIRED tests keyed on it, so those p-values could not be recomputed from stored data. The parser fix was a genuine no-op everywhere except Qwen GPQA +8 (Llama invalid 0.0000 in 8 of 9 cells; MMLU invalid 0.0 for both models). Generalizable lesson: **"the model was re-run" does NOT imply "all of that model's tasks were re-run"** — the Qwen re-run covered GPQA only, leaving Qwen MMLU on the old schema for a day. Check the header per FILE, not per model. Two fixed bugs: the new summary fields were missing from the CSV `fieldnames` (`ValueError` at the very END of a multi-hour run, after JSON and per-sample CSV were written), and `--skip_orig` loaded `orig_rows` but never carried them into the rewritten JSON, so the paired baseline was lost after one use. **Reproducibility boundary: `temperature=1.0` with no fixed seed — statistical trends reproduce, exact numbers do not byte-match.**
- `get_answer_gpqa_cot_delay.py` (`run_gpqa_cot_delay.sh`) — **delay discounting**: model decides when to commit (`Final Answer: X`); CoT token length before commit = delay tolerance. Out: `${BASE_DIR}/${MODEL}/gpqa_delay/`.
- `get_action_effort_choice.py` (`run_effort_choice.sh`) — **effort-based choice**: GSM8K (easy) vs. MATH (hard) free selection. Includes an all-layer α=±1 (`1-1-33`) condition alongside the best-layer ±4.
<!-- Why: the CLOSED verdict lived only in deep pv10c/pv11 bullets, so a reader skimming the suite could
reopen a line that five intervention classes already failed to move.
Evidence: AdaBandit.md §5 + Protocol Lineage; PV11 pre-registered termination rule.
Scope: the whole Bandit line. Reopening needs a NEW protocol, not a re-run. -->
- **⛔ THE ENTIRE BANDIT LINE (pv1–PV11) IS CLOSED — do not start new Bandit experiments.** Per the PV11 pre-registered termination rule, **five** intervention classes (Stage-1 α, choice history, Beta calculator, competitor cue, controlled evidence state) each raised the model's *recognition* of uncertainty without moving *acquisition*. Bandit's role in the paper is **boundary evidence for directed exploration** — α changes policy stance / commitment / decision sharpness, but is NOT a general exploration controller. Do not re-run, extend seeds, or iterate the prompt: with events this sparse that is chasing noise. Conclusions are frozen in `AdaBandit.md` §5 + the Protocol Lineage table. The detail below is a **historical record**, kept because later protocols reuse its seed banks, gate rules and analysis 口径.
- `get_answer_bandit.py` (`run_bandit_llama3.sh` / `run_bandit_qwen25.sh`) — **multi-armed bandit**: explore/exploit over `--num_runs`×`--num_rounds` (30×50; `--pilot` = 2 runs × 3 α, rounds stay 50). De-roled port of the EVOLvE/BanditBench boutique MAB (`~/Documents/Benchmark/EVOLvE`, Nie et al. ICML 2025): explore/exploit paragraph verbatim, persona stripped, own local `build_prompt` (NOT `utils.build_prompt`), **bare-string — `use_chat` is not implemented at all**, so steering sits on the bare distribution the NMD mask was extracted in.
  - **⚠️ ALL PRE-2026-07-28 BANDIT RESULTS ARE VOID — do not cite them, they cannot be salvaged offline (no raw text was stored).** Two design faults were found and fixed in `get_answer_bandit.py`: **(A) position leakage** — `shuffle_arms()` shuffled arm NAMES then zipped against the descending prob list, so the best arm sat at display position 1 for EVERY seed (verified seeds 0–29); **(B) permissive parser** — `parse_choice()` returned the first arm name appearing anywhere, so a reply that merely echoed the option list scored as a VALID choice of the BEST arm. Together they made OptFrac indistinguishable from a first-option bias. **The published inverted-U (plateau α=0…+6, peak +2, +8 collapse 0.515) rests on this data and is retired**; `AdaDopamine.md` §3.2 and any cross-task table listing Bandit's working point must be re-derived, not merely re-labelled. Post-fix: `shuffle_arms` shuffles name→prob and display order independently (`position_of_best(seed)` exposed for the counterbalance check); `parse_choice` is valid only on EXACTLY one match, returning `n_matched` so "said nothing" (0) and "restated the menu" (>1) separate offline; per-round `raws`/`valid_flags`/`n_matched` and `seed`/`best_position`/`arm_order` are now stored (**the `seed` field's presence is the pre/post-fix marker** — the 15 legacy dirs lack it).
  - **pv1–pv5 operational detail is RETIRED to `AdaBandit.md` §1.4 (the four inherited constraints + pv5's frozen verdict, moved there verbatim) and the Protocol Lineage table (results).** What survives here is only what a later protocol still depends on. **All pre-2026-07-28 results are VOID** (see the bullet above). The two design faults and their fixes live in `get_answer_bandit.py`'s `PROTOCOL_VERSION` comment. Four facts that later protocols inherit: (a) the **counterbalanced seed set `0 3 4 9 37`** (best arm at display positions 2,4,5,3,1, five distinct best names) — the earlier 2-seed pilot accidentally shared BOTH name and position, which is bad luck at n=2, **not** a `shuffle_arms` defect, so do not "fix" the shuffle; (b) `parse_choice_exact` is **strict on purpose** and its `n_matched` separates three failure signatures — `(invalid,0)` nothing recognisable, `(invalid,1)` **named an arm but wrapped it in prose**, `(invalid,>1)` restated the menu; (c) `fallback_rng` is a continuous stream seeded `1_000_000 + seed`, so cells with different invalid counts draw different fallback arms at the same round — reward draws stay paired but ITT `opt_frac` absorbs a little fallback luck (**unfixed**; stratify absolute OptFrac by `best_position`); (d) the resume key needs an `iface` segment or reusing one `ans_file` across changed interface flags silently returns the old row. **pv5's frozen verdict:** E-direct's failure is an **early-outcome-dependent greedy confirmation loop** — whichever arm earns the FIRST reward=1 becomes a self-reinforcing incumbent; given equalized trial counts the model tracks the empirical-best set correctly (adherence ≈0.93), so the gap is a missing uncertainty bonus, not integration failure. E-CoT's marginal value is **UNESTABLISHED**, and invalid rounds may **not** be offline-patched (a differing fallback pull changes every downstream state).
  - **pv6 (F-reference) — the 2026-08 clean-slate redesign. SUPERSEDED as the main line (pv6 → pv7 → pv8 → pv9; see the pv9 bullet below), but still the source of the frozen competence gate, seed banks and algorithmic baselines every later protocol reuses.** Built after the pv5 verdict above; it does NOT continue that line, and nothing here is comparable to a pv1–pv5 cell (different environment, prompt, decoding, reward model, metrics, output tree, CSV schema). Design doc: `AdaBandit.md` §3 (renamed 2026-08-11 from `BanditExperiment_LiteratureReview.md`; older refs are stale) — read it before changing anything.
    - **Framing constraint on any two-scope result (settled 2026-08-05, binding):** a working "rationale −4 + action +4" combination would show RSN composes into an effective **phased division-of-labour controller** (階段分工控制器). It would **NOT** show that a single α has dopamine-like properties — the same episode uses two OPPOSITE directions. Keep that boundary in any writeup; the `both`-stage +4 data (margin down, non-novel churn up, persistence damaged) is *not* evidence of effective exploration, so do not assume action-side +4 will help Hard. The `{0,−4}×{0,+4}` 2×2 with `Δ_interaction = Y(−4,+4) − Y(−4,0) − Y(0,+4) + Y(0,0)` is the test, and it is **deferred**: it needs separate `rationale_alpha`/`action_alpha` parameters that do not exist yet, and `both(−4)` cannot serve as the `(−4,0)` cell.
    - **Framing (settled): this measures a CAPABILITY BOUNDARY and whether α moves it — not "make the task work".** Krishnamurthy et al. (NeurIPS 2024) found exactly ONE configuration that explores successfully across all their experiments (GPT-4 BSSĈ0); Llama2 failed its entire restricted sweep. A 7–8B model failing is the literature's expectation, so the competence gate is **pre-registered**, not chosen after seeing numbers.
    - **Files.** `bandit_reference.py` = the shared module (environments, frozen seed banks, per-arm reward tapes, F-reference prompts, rationale sanitization, candidate utils, reference metrics, Greedy/Oracle/Random). `bandit_pv6_episode.py` = the two-stage episode runner. `get_answer_bandit.py --reference_environment {easy,hard,native_floor}` dispatches to it (pv1–pv5 paths byte-unchanged; the flag REJECTS pv1–pv5 prompt/parser flags rather than ignoring them). `run_bandit_reference.sh` = launcher, `run_bandit_pv6.sh` = server driver (phase-gated). `run_bandit_algorithmic_baseline.py --reference_environment …` = the pv6 baseline mode, which imports the SAME module so "baseline and model faced the same environment" is true by construction. Two formal wrappers split Track A by interface: `run_bandit_pv6_bare.sh` (check → run → **gate**) and `run_bandit_pv6_chat.sh` (check → run, **no gate** — chat is never gate-eligible); both take `--yes` plus optional `--easy-only`/`--hard-only`, and both write per-PID log names so two GPUs can run in parallel.
      - **Launcher mechanics are retired to `AdaBandit.md` §3 (the line is CLOSED, so step selectors and smoke steps are dead weight).** One trap generalises to EVERY server script here: **`PY` defaults to `python`, not `python3.10`** — the server conda env names its interpreter `python`, while `python3.10` is the LOCAL analysis-box convention and does not exist there, where it exits **127 before anything runs** and a background `nohup` job dies silently. After launching, `cat` the log immediately rather than assuming it started.
    - **Environments** (μ\*=0.5+Δ/2, μ=0.5−Δ/2): `easy` K=4 .75/.25×3 T=100; `hard` K=5 .60/.40×4 T=100 — both competence-gate eligible. `native_floor` K=2 .70/.30 T=50 is a minimal stochastic-adaptation diagnostic ONLY: not a competence anchor, no B1 α experiment.
    - **Steering semantics: `--steering_scope {action,both}` (2026-08-04).** WHERE α lands inside a pass is frozen for both scopes — **once, at that pass's last prefill token, `prefill_only`, `tail_len=1`, decode never steered**. What the flag selects is WHICH passes get it. Stage 2 always injects at the tail of `Choice: Button`; the prompt must end there and nothing may follow it. Both stages temperature=0 (`generate` and `regenerate(prefill_only=True)` are both `do_sample=False` at temperature 0, so the scopes differ ONLY by hook registration).
      - `action` (the ORIGINAL pv6 semantics, now the mechanism **ABLATION**): Stage 1 gets no α — it calls `vc.generate`, which registers no hooks at all. Measures whether α moves the arm logits GIVEN a fixed rationale, i.e. an action readout.
      - `both` (the **B1 MAIN experiment**): Stage 1 is also steered, via `vc.regenerate(prefill_only=True, prefill_tail_len=1)`. α then reaches the choice by two routes — α→rationale text→action and α→action logits — which is what "does α move the whole information-seeking policy" requires. Do NOT widen this to decode-wide steering; prefill-only is the RSN convention and widening changes the intervention's definition.
      - **α=0 is identical under both scopes and must stay that way.** `diff_mtx is None` at α=0, so NO hook is registered in either pass — "unsteered" is deliberately not the same code path as "steered by zero". This is what makes the stored Track A α=0 cells reusable as B1's α=0 without a re-run; do not "unify" the two branches. Tests assert the trajectory and prompt chain are identical across scopes at α=0.
      - **Resume key**: the scope segment `sc{scope}{STEERING_SCOPE_VERSION}` (currently `scbothsv1`) is appended **only for a non-default scope** — the same asymmetry as `ut`, and for the same reason: stored Track A rows carry no scope segment, so emitting `scaction` would make every completed α=0 cell look unrun. `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays `pv6`: scope changes the intervention, not the environment/prompt/scoring/seed bank/metrics. `STEERING_SCOPE_VERSION` exists so a LATER change to how `both` injects (tail>1, decode-wide, different anchor) cannot be mistaken for the same intervention.
      - **One `--ans_file` per α, always.** The pv6 detail JSON is named `bandit_pv6_{env}_{size}_{TOP}_{ls}_{le}.json` — no α and no scope in it — so two scopes in one dir overwrite each other; and `summary_{model}_{size}.csv` is per-`ans_file`, so parallel α processes sharing a dir would interleave appends and race the header migration. B1 dirs: `pv6_{easy,hard}_bare_both_{am4,ap4}`.
    - **`steering_fires` = OBSERVED injection evidence, in SITES.** `VicundaModel.steering_fire_count(reset=False)` counts one per **(steered layer, sequence, token position) that received a non-zero add** — deliberately NOT hook calls. `diff_matrices` is full-length (all 32 decoder layers, zero rows outside the band) and every layer registers a hook, and a K-candidate action pass is ONE batched forward, so a call-counter reports the same number for any layer count and any K (3200 for both stages on Llama3-8B) and proves nothing. With L steered layers: `regenerate(prefill_only, tail=t, B prompts) → L·B·t`; `regenerate_logits_teacher_forcing(K prompts) → L·K`. **L is 9, not 10, for the standard `11-20` band** — `utils.decoder_layer_range(11,20) = range(10,19)`, half-open like every other layer range in the repo (the "10 layers" figure elsewhere in this file is the HS-storage set: middle + final). **A pv6 episode at T=100 must therefore read `rationale=900` and `action=3600` (easy K=4) / `4500` (hard K=5)** — verified against the B1 smoke, which read exactly 900/3600. Deviations are diagnostic: 3200 = zero-rows counted (the site fix is not live), 100 = only one layer injecting, 0 = hook never fired; and `action == rationale` means the K factor is missing, so the counter cannot distinguish the two passes. The record carries both `steered_rationale` (configured intent) and `steering_fires` (observed) — **they can disagree only if there is a bug, which is why both exist**.
    - **Two-stage constrained choice ⇒ `invalid_rate` is STRUCTURALLY 0.** No free-text parser, no random fallback. A nonzero value means a bug, not a bad model.
    - **Chat mode: role structure is frozen** — user turn = task state only, assistant turn = the model's own rationale CONTINUED into the anchor (no `<|eot_id|>` between them). Feeding the rationale back inside the user turn would present the model's reasoning as if the environment said it. Chat also REQUIRES stripping the template's serialized BOS: `regenerate_logits_teacher_forcing` hardcodes `add_special_tokens=True` (llms.py:505), so without stripping the ids read `[128000, 128000, …]`. `audit_prompt_tokens` raises on double-BOS and on a prompt not ending at the anchor, and runs EVERY round.
    - **Per-arm reward tapes**, not one stream advanced per round: the n-th pull of arm *a* is the same latent outcome for every policy sharing a seed, so a cross-α difference cannot be reward luck on that arm.
    - **`bandit_pv6_baseline_manifest.json` is the FROZEN gate basis** (seed banks + bank_report + Random/Greedy/Oracle point estimates + bootstrap CIs), built before any model ran. `freeze_bandit_baseline.py --check` recomputes and diffs; a MISMATCH means the basis is not reproducible on that machine and **nothing downstream is citable**. Compare in round-tripped JSON form — `position_counts` has int keys and JSON has none.
    - **Seed banks are counterbalanced by a Latin-rectangle construction** (position × identity cross table, not just the two marginals): Easy 16/16 cells max repeat 2, Hard 20/20 distinct. A greedy first-come scan CANNOT reach this — do not "simplify" it back. Smoke seeds are disjoint from the formal bank; `run_bandit_reference.sh` hardcodes both and `test_bandit_reference.py` asserts they match the manifest (a copy can drift).
    - **Gate point estimates are seed-bank-sensitive at N=20** — Easy Greedy SuffFail moved 0.10→0.25 when the bank was fixed, and its bootstrap CI is [0.100, 0.450]. The mechanical gate uses point estimates (per the pre-registration), but always report the interval; rule 2's basis is by contrast very tight (Greedy KxMinFrac CI [0.040, 0.040] vs Random ~0.79).
    - **`evaluate_competence_gate.py` was frozen BEFORE Track A ran.** Pre-registering four rules is not the same as fixing their implementation, so the free parameters are settled in-file: `half = T//2`; **SuffFail(T/2) is a SUFFIX `[half,T)` but KxMinFrac(T/2) is a PREFIX `[0,half)`** (the easiest thing to get silently wrong); "decreasing" compares exactly two points; strict `<`/`>` so ties fail; 1/K from the environment's K; missing/duplicate/extra seeds are a hard ERROR, never a silent subset; trajectory length ≠ env horizon is a hard ERROR. Rule 1's non-novel-churn clause is deliberately **NOT mechanized** (a threshold invented there would be the post-hoc freedom the file exists to prevent) — churn is computed, reported, and flagged for human reading. Paired bootstrap is uncertainty only and never overrides. `--selftest` validates discriminating power against synthetic fixtures (Thompson passes all four, Random fails rule 2, Greedy fails rule 1, Oracle fails rule 2 as a single-arm policy). **Do not edit this file now that data exists.**
    - **Order is frozen and gated**: manifest check → smoke (Easy/Hard × bare/chat × 3 seeds, protocol correctness only) → `A0_BARE` → `A0_CHAT` → gate verdict → only then B1 α∈{−4,0,+4}. The competence gate is judged on **reference-bare ONLY** (the NMD mask is bare-string; chat is the literature comparator). If neither bare condition passes, an α sweep is **failure-mode characterization** — the words capability-effect / rescue / improvement are not available.
    - **GATE VERDICT (2026-08-04, N=20, frozen): Easy-bare PASS (4/4 rules), Hard-bare FAIL (rule 1 only).** Easy-bare is therefore the B1 competence anchor and α there may be discussed as moving a competent policy; **Hard-bare α is failure-mode characterization only** — the forbidden words above apply to hard, not to easy. Numbers: Easy SuffFail 0.150 vs Greedy 0.250, KxMinFrac 0.046, adherence 0.905, late OptFrac 0.739. Hard SuffFail **0.450 vs Greedy 0.350** (the only failing rule), KxMinFrac 0.030, adherence 0.801, late OptFrac 0.471.
      - **Hard's failure is single-point and diagnosable: exploration COVERAGE, not value integration.** 10/20 episodes never tried all 5 arms (3 pulled only ONE arm for all 100 rounds), 7/20 never pulled the best arm at all. But *conditional on having discovered the best arm*, Hard's late OptFrac is 0.725 vs Easy's 0.739 — indistinguishable — and rule 3 (adherence 0.801) passes. So the model reads its own data correctly; it just does not go get the data. This is the same mechanism pv5 hit (given equalized trial counts the model tracks the empirical-best set; what is missing is an uncertainty bonus), now reproduced in a clean reference environment.
      - **Hard's rule-1 gap is inside the noise: paired bootstrap `suff_fail_vs_greedy = +0.100, CI [−0.150, +0.350]` straddles 0.** The gate judges point estimates per the pre-registration, so FAIL stands and must not be re-judged — but the correct wording is "**did not pass the gate**", NOT "significantly worse than Greedy". Always report the interval alongside.
      - **Easy-chat also passes all four rules — but chat is NOT gate-eligible and this is diagnostic only** (`evaluate_competence_gate.py` prints its own not-a-competence-anchor header when handed a chat dir). It passes by a DIFFERENT mechanism than bare: chat explores more (arms discovered 4.00/4 vs bare 3.60/4) but converges worse (adherence 0.559 vs 0.905, late OptFrac 0.506 vs 0.739, churn 0.243 vs 0.059, regret 27.9 vs 14.8) — the mirror image of Hard-bare. When citing "chat explores more", use `arms_discovered`/`churn_rate`; do NOT use rule 1, whose bare-vs-chat CIs overlap heavily. Hard-chat had not been run as of 2026-08-04, so the interface contrast is incomplete.
    - **Hard-chat (run 2026-08-05) completes the interface 2×2 and confirms the mechanism split: chat RELOCATES the failure, it does not rescue it.** Hard-chat fails rule 1 only, at *exactly* Hard-bare's 0.450 — same single failing rule, same value. Chat fixes coverage on both environments (Hard arms_discovered 3.35→5.00/5) but collapses convergence (Hard adherence 0.801→0.396; Easy 0.905→0.559). So neither interface gets both halves: bare converges without exploring, chat explores without converging. Do NOT read Easy-chat's 4/4 pass as "chat is better" — it is not gate-eligible and it passes by the opposite mechanism.
    - **B1 RESULT, Easy-bare both-stage α=±4 (N=20 paired seeds, 2026-08-05): +4 DAMAGES the policy; −4 is behaviourally null.** Full tables + interpretation live in `AdaDopamine.md` §3.2.5 — cite there. The load-bearing decomposition: **+4's damage is to PERSISTENCE, not to discovery.** 96.6% of its extra switching lands on already-tried arms (`nonnovel_churn_rate` .059→.159, p=.0014) and 99.6% of its extra non-greedy rounds occur *after* the best arm was already found; `best_never_tried` stays 0/20 and `arms_discovered` barely moves. Adherence .905→.672 (p=.0064), late OptFrac .739→.579, regret 14.8→21.4. **This is why "+α = more exploration" is the wrong reading** — the model is not going and getting new information, it is failing to hold onto what it already found. −4 is null on every outcome metric but NOT inert: it shifts the candidate distribution and the rationale text, so report it as "behaviourally null, distributionally active", never as "no effect".
      - **No inverted-U here — only a right arm.** Three α points on a ceiling-adjacent baseline cannot show a peak. Easy-bare α=0 is already near this task's ceiling (late OptFrac .739, adherence .905, `best_never_tried` 0/20), leaving −α no headroom — structurally the same baseline-ceiling artifact as Qwen's MMLU betting cell. **The dilemma this creates is real and unresolved:** the environment that passed the gate has no improvement room; the one with room (Hard) did not pass.
      - **`analyze_bandit_pv6_alpha.py` (in `RoleAnswer/`) is the FROZEN parser for §3.2.5** — every number in that section has exactly one definition there. It fails closed: `attest()` derives each cell's expected `steering_fires` from that cell's OWN config via `expected_fires(alpha, scope, k, horizon)` and raises `SystemExit` on mismatch, so behaviour is never read off a cell whose intervention is unverified. Attestation runs for EVERY `--part`, not just `--part attest` (a single-part call is the normal way to pull one table and must not bypass the check); `--skip-attest` exists for debugging only and prints a warning. Two口径 that are load-bearing: pairing is **by seed** (all cells share one frozen bank → paired Wilcoxon, never Mann-Whitney), and "identical text vs α=0" is computed on **matched state** (rounds with an identical `(choices, feedbacks)` prefix), not same-round-index — the latter attributes an input difference to steering once trajectories diverge and reads 11.9% where matched-state reads 52.4%. `_is_emp_best` is **tie-tolerant to match `evaluate_competence_gate.py:115`**; a bare `argmax` reads ~0.3pp low on adherence and disagrees with the frozen gate — do not "simplify" it. Exploration keywords are reported under TWO regexes on purpose (strict `explor*` = 23.5%, expanded = 33.9%) because these are post-hoc statistics and one keyword list must not silently carry a mechanism conclusion.
      - **`--cells` MERGES into the default three-cell set, it does not replace it** — the intended use is overriding a known key (`ap4=…`). An unrecognized key adds a 4th cell, and `load_cells` then rejects it on the seed-intersection check. For a one-off (e.g. an N=1 smoke) call `attest({...})` directly instead. Paths are relative to `PV6_ROOT`, which already ends in `bandit/pv6`.
    - **`B1_ACTION` = the mechanism ABLATION (`--steering_scope action`, Easy, α=±4).** Asks whether α still moves the choice when the rationale is fixed, i.e. an action-side readout. Launcher steps `B1_ACTION_SMOKE` / `B1_ACTION` / `B1_ACTION_EASY_AM4` / `B1_ACTION_EASY_AP4`. **The acceptance check is `steering_fires == {rationale: 0, action: 3600}`** — `rationale: 0` proves Stage 1 registered no hook at all (genuinely unsteered, not steered-by-zero); the B1 action smoke read exactly that. **Separate output dirs are MANDATORY, not tidiness**: the pv6 detail JSON name carries neither α nor scope, and action's resume key carries no scope segment either (it is the default scope, so `iface` has no `scbothsv1`) — writing these into `pv6_easy_bare/` would OVERWRITE the Track A α=0 cell the competence gate is built on.
    - **Cost**: 2 forward passes per round. Full Track A = Easy+Hard × bare+chat × 20 seeds = **80 episodes / 8000 rounds**; the "40 episodes" figure is ONE interface.
  - **pv7 (2026-08-05) — the interface rebuild that superseded pv6. Itself superseded by pv8/pv9, but it defines the token/anchor invariants and the Stage-1/Stage-2 split every later protocol inherits.** pv6 is NOT void: it stays citable as a **boundary finding** (what α does on an interface that truncates the rationale, contradicts itself at Stage 2, drifts the option display, and leaves a label prior uncontrolled). But its α numbers cannot be pooled with pv7's, and pv7 needs its OWN α=0 competence anchor before any α claim. Files: `bandit_pv7.py` (prompts/state, pure — no model), `test_bandit_pv7.py`, `freeze_pv7_states.py` + `bandit_pv7_frozen_states.json`, `eval_pv7_frozen_states.py`. pv6 files are byte-unchanged.
    - **Four pv6 defects it fixes, all measured not assumed:** (1) **rationale truncation** — 74–93% of pv6 rationales end mid-sentence at the 64-token cap and only 224/2000 reach `## Step 2` (9 finish it), so what Stage 2 received was mostly a restatement of the state table with the explore/exploit weighing cut off; (2) **Stage 2 self-contradiction** — pv6 rebuilds `state + _RATIONALE_INSTRUCTION`, so "Do not state a final choice yet" is still ~40 tokens above the anchor that demands a choice; (3) **option-display drift** — pv6's TRIED/UNTRIED split moves arms as they get tried, so **24/120 frozen slots (slot 96/120 = 80.0%, dedup 87/107 = 81.3%)** show an order differing from the run's fixed `arm_order`; pv7's single OPTIONS table is 100%; (4) **label prior** — pv6 round-1 picks "Button A" ~95% of the time by LABEL while display position spreads {1:10, 2:4, 3:5, 4:1}, i.e. a label prior, NOT a position bias, so the counterbalanced seed bank does not neutralize it (it balances position). Handle by statistical control (label fixed effects, `α × Button A` interaction); candidate-prior calibration is a scaffold and belongs in an ablation only.
    - **Anchor design is the load-bearing change, and it is about RSN alignment, not aesthetics.** Both stages now end in ONE literal ASCII space: `Evidence: ` (Stage 1) and `Choose Button: ` (Stage 2). For the Llama-3.1 tokenizer that tail is **token 220**, the same decision-bottleneck token the RSN mask was extracted at (`Answer: ` in the MMLU template). pv6 instead ended Stage 2 at `Choice: Button` → ` Button` (6739) and Stage 1 at the period of "…final choice yet." — an asymmetry, not an inert site. **Correction to an earlier reading: injecting at that period is NOT ineffective** — on round 0, where the state is byte-identical across cells, `both(+4)` changed the rationale in 20/20 seeds (`both(−4)`: 11/20). A period's hidden state carries the whole context; the real defect was that the two stages anchored at semantically different places.
    - **Candidates are BARE `A`/`B`/`C`/`D`/`E` = token 32/33/34/35/36** (pv6 used `' A'` = 362). This is forced by the trailing space and is itself a reason pv7 is a new protocol. **The concatenation must happen at the ID level.** `llms.py:527-537` (`regenerate_logits_teacher_forcing`) tokenizes prompt and answer separately and concatenates id lists, giving `[…, 220, 32]` — correct. Building the sequence as the STRING `prompt + candidate` instead lets BPE merge `220 + A` back into `' A'`: the sequence is one token SHORTER, the scored token becomes 362, and the injection site silently slides to the colon (25). It does not raise. `bandit_pv7.audit_id_level_continuation()` fails closed on all of this and is the negative control.
    - **Two silent-corruption hazards worth knowing:** `.rstrip()` on a pv7 prompt moves the tail token from 220 to `:` (25) — which is why "the trailing space must survive" is an assertion, not a comment; and a stray DOUBLE space is a single token 256, not `220,220`, so the rationale (never the prompt) is `rstrip`ped before the anchor is appended.
    - **Anchor collision is OBSERVED, never cleaned (settled 2026-08-05).** pv7 invites the policy to name a button, so the model can emit `Choose Button:` itself, putting two anchors in the Stage 2 prompt. The injection site is still correct (verified: tail stays `[…,25,220]`, boundary `[220,32]`), but a rationale that already committed gives Stage 2 a self-cue that would inflate the action-follows-rationale readout. `bandit_pv7.rationale_format_flags()` records `rationale_contains_action_anchor` / `rationale_contains_evidence_anchor` / `empty_rationale` / `starts_with_redundant_evidence` and **nothing is dropped** — report the metric overall AND on the collision-free subset, because deleting collisions creates a selection bias. A materially non-zero rate disqualifies the PROMPT VERSION; it is not repaired in post-processing. Do NOT pre-emptively switch to `Selection: ` (also token 220) — that trades one possible collision for another and adds a variable before any collision rate has been measured. Once trajectories run, the anchor is frozen.
    - **Frozen-state bank: 120 slots, 107 unique histories — two analysis units, do not mix.** 20 seeds × 6 state types (round_1 / first_partial_tried_untried / after_first_positive_reward / after_first_two_consecutive_zero_rewards / round_50 / round_100), sampled from stored α=0 pv6 trajectories. The 13 duplicates are all `first_partial_tried_untried` coinciding with an event type. **(a)** the six per-type tables keep all 20 slots each (one history answers a different diagnostic question under each type); **(b)** any POOLED statistic deduplicates on `state_fingerprint` (a verified bijection with `(seed, history, arm_order)`) and reports **n=107, not 120**. A bare percentage with no denominator is a reporting bug — this is exactly how 80.0% and 81.3% can both be right for the same fact.
    - **`eval_pv7_frozen_states.py` = Phase 3, a paired INTERFACE comparison (not a wording ablation).** Three arms on the same frozen states: `p0_pv6_interface` (the REAL pv6 path — its prompt, sanitizer, `Choice: Button` anchor and `' A'` candidates), `p1_structural`, `p2_light_hint` (P1 + one weak-evidence sentence). **P0 must never be "aligned" to token 220 or bare candidates** — that would measure wording instead of the interface change actually being made. Consequence to state whenever citing them: margin / norm_entropy differences between P0 and P1/P2 **include** the anchor and candidate-tokenization delta. Held constant across arms: the frozen state, `temperature=0`, `max_new_tokens=64` (module constants, deliberately not per-arm). Validity is judged against each arm's OWN protocol — requiring pv6 to end on 220 would report a spurious failure for an invariant it never claimed. Cost is reported per stage. `--dry_run` needs no GPU (prompt construction + validity only); `--model_dir` defaults to the HF repo id `meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct`, matching `run_bandit_reference.sh:119` — there is no filesystem model dir. ~360 generations + 360 batched scorings.
    - **Selection rule (frozen): choose the prompt on validity / evidence grounding / rationale completion / cost ONLY, never on true-best outcome.** The frozen states were sampled from α=0 pv6 trajectories, so selecting by reward fits the prompt to its own history.
    - **Grounding metrics separate FACT from EPISTEMICS.** `any_grounding_error` covers only mechanically checkable errors (fabricated reward/trial counts, wrong empirical rate, an UNTRIED arm described as observed, and at Round 1 any reward claim at all — nothing has been observed yet, which is why Round-1 hallucination is reported on its own 20-state denominator). Whether 1 trial may be called "well-supported" is a judgement, so `support_overclaim` is recorded DESCRIPTIVELY and stays out of `any_grounding_error`. Grounding is computed on BOTH `raw` (primary: what the model generated) and `clean` (what Stage 2 saw) — for P0 these differ, since pv6's sanitizer deletes whole `Choice:` lines, and a clean-only reading understates the generation tendency.
    - **`policy_*` fields are HEURISTIC post-hoc descriptors, not pre-registered outcomes or gate metrics.** `policy_target_source ∈ {policy_segment, full_text_fallback, none}`: the headline action-follows-policy uses `policy_segment` WITH a clear explore/exploit stance, because the fallback picks the last button named anywhere and will happily return a button mentioned in the *Evidence* half (verified: "Evidence: Button C looks best but Button D untried; Policy: explore Button D" → segment correctly yields D, fallback would yield C). Always print the parse rate beside the follow rate — a high rate over a small easily-parsed subset is not a general result.
    - **P1b = the SELECTED Stage 1 interface (Phase 3 follow-up).** P1 and P2 both failed selection: P1 left 39.3% of policies naming no button and 17.8% of generations continuing into the next round's prompt; P2's weak-evidence hint became a policy prior and pushed the model to call tried arms untried. `PROMPT_P1B` = P1's structure + explicit termination + a mandatory named button, and deliberately **no** decision hint. Run it with `--arms p1b_terminated`; it is excluded from `ALL_ARMS` so a bare re-run cannot silently overwrite the frozen three-arm result. Result: policy parse rate 60.7% → **100%**, lowest grounding error, cost unchanged (63.99 vs 64 tokens). **But `native_ends_after_policy=79.4%` does NOT mean the model stopped** — 119/120 rationales still hit the token cap, and 106/120 clean texts carry trailing hashtag spray (`#100daysofbandits …`) inside the retained Policy line. The correct wording is **"parser-assisted interface succeeded, native stopping failed"**.
    - **`eval_pv7_stage2_ablation.py` = the Stage-2 mechanism diagnostic that located the real bottleneck (2026-08-06).** Three Stage 1 rewrites (P1→P2→P1b) moved Button-A coverage by ~6pp (McNemar p=0.77, n_discordant=12) while lifting parse rate 60.7%→100%, so the bottleneck was never Stage 1 expression quality. This re-scores the frozen P1b rationales **verbatim** (no Stage 1 re-run) under `{S0, S1, S_maskpolicy} × 4 cyclic OPTIONS rotations` = 1440 batched scorings, ~10 min. **S1 = "Follow the Policy above and select one button."** — names no button, supplies no strategy, only asks Stage 2 to use the model's own Policy. **RESULT: non-A-policy overridden by A fell 54.3% → 1.9%** (McNemar n=428, discordant **178/0**, p<1e-4), Button C follow 10.9% → **95.7%**, and choice became rotation-invariant (**0/107** states change across rotations vs S0's 14/107). Margin ROSE (+0.83) while entropy FELL (−0.10): S1 moves probability mass onto the target rather than flattening the distribution. Frozen design points: rotation moves ROWS only (the letter is the arm's identity); the rationale is never re-ordered or rewritten (a `Button C` landing in different rows across rotations IS the intervention); `S_maskpolicy` uses **S0's** instruction, since pairing a removed Policy with "Follow the Policy above" would demand adherence to a policy that is not there. No true-best-arm or reward metric is computed — the states were sampled from α=0 pv6 trajectories, so outcome-based selection would fit the prompt to its own history.
    - **Label effect ≈ 6× the row-position effect (balanced design, supersedes the observational estimate).** Line above records pv6's "label prior, NOT position bias"; pv7's 4-rotation design confirms it WITH magnitudes: centered log-prob span by label **4.73** (A +3.09, B −0.09, C −1.64, D −1.36) vs by row position **0.76** (1: +0.62 … 4: −0.14), and choice share is flat across rows (25/26/23/26%). **Do not claim the two are collinear** — in the pv7 bank Button A sits in row 1 in only 54/120 states and `arm_order` takes 12 distinct values, so they are separable; the earlier observational row-span of 2.16 was inflated by that imbalance. Reading position off the `scores` dict key order is the trap: that dict is always A,B,C,D, NOT display order — use `arm_order`. **`label FE` must be estimated controlling for `is_policy_target`**, or target-frequency imbalance masquerades as prior: S1's raw label span 3.08 drops to **+0.30 for non-target Button A** (vs S0's +2.17, mask's +3.39) — i.e. S1 nearly eliminates A's advantage exactly when A is not the policy target.
    - **Policy plausibility (mechanism description, NOT an S1 gate).** Under the three-way口径 — (A) `EXPLOIT` → a well-tried clearly-worse arm, (B) late (≥r50) → a well-tried worse arm, (C) `EXPLORE` → a low-n arm (**not** an error) — the 79 clear parseable policies give **A=0, B=0, C=20**, and 37/37 well-tried EXPLOIT policies name exactly the empirical-best arm (max gap 0.0). So S1's ~99% follow rate is executing policies that are themselves sound. This cannot distinguish "obeys sound policies" from "obeys unconditionally" (there is no unsound policy to serve as a counterexample), so it stays descriptive.
    - **CONSEQUENCE for α, decided in advance: pv7 moves the interesting α to Stage 1.** S1 leaves Stage 2 ~1.9% room to overrule the Policy, so pv7's **action-only cell is expected to be near-null, and that null reads as "the executor is robust", NOT "α does not affect the action"**. pv6's action-vs-both contrast is **not transferable** — it was informative precisely because pv6's Stage 2 DID overrule the rationale. Accordingly α is split into `rationale_alpha` / `action_alpha` rather than inheriting `steering_scope`, which cannot express the `(α, 0)` cell that is now the primary experiment.
    - **`bandit_pv7_episode.py` + `run_bandit_pv7_episodes.py` + `run_bandit_pv7.sh` + `evaluate_competence_gate_pv7.py` = the pv7 trajectory line.** The runner is standalone (importing it must not pull in `bandit_pv6_episode` — asserted by subprocess in `test_bandit_pv7_episode.py`); the driver is thin, reusing only model/mask loading. Both stages see ONE fixed OPTIONS order — **rotation belongs to the ablation only**, never to a real episode. **Output files are `bandit_pv7_*.json` in a pv7-only tree and are NEVER disguised as `bandit_pv6_*.json`**: the frozen gate loader globs pv6 names and is deliberately blind to pv7, so `evaluate_competence_gate_pv7.py` supplies a pv7 loader and calls the SAME frozen `evaluate()` — the RULES are imported, never copied. `environment.name` stays `reference_easy` because the environment genuinely did not change, which is what keeps the frozen seed banks and algorithmic baselines valid. Per-round records store Stage 1 `policy_target`/`policy_stance` and Stage 2 `action`/`action_follows_policy` separately, so a gate failure has an address: **decision quality** vs **execution consistency** (both reported by the wrapper, neither a gate rule).
    - **pv7 fail-closed engineering (each was a real gap, fixed before the first GPU run):** `steering_fires` is verified **per episode** against `expected_fires` (Easy L=9/K=4/T=100: rationale-only `{900, 0}`, action-only `{0, 3600}`), and **`None` is a FAILURE, not a skip** — `VicundaModel.steering_fire_count` is unconditional (llms.py:814), so `None` can only mean a stale server `llms.py`; sync it rather than bypassing the check, and do NOT add an escape flag. The gate wrapper's `all_unsteered` likewise fails on a missing count (filtering `None` away would make the one unverifiable case report True vacuously; `all([])` is also True, so the empty case is handled). The **resume key hashes the seed CONTENT**, not just `len(seeds)` — a count alone would let the smoke bank (`6 12 13`) and any other 3-seed set resume into each other and return episodes from different environments under this cell's name; it is order-insensitive but content-sensitive. Two different non-zero alphas raise rather than silently reusing one magnitude. The Policy parser is a **hard dependency** with no fallback: `parse_policy` lives in `bandit_pv7.py` (pure, model-free), because a fail-open parser emits trajectories that look valid, satisfy the gate's read-only contract, and cannot be analysed. `POLICY_PARSER_VERSION` is metadata only (it re-labels stored text); changing the **extractor** that Stage 2 reads must bump the protocol/resume key instead.
    - **pv7 is LLAMA3-ONLY.** Token 220 and candidates 32–35 are Llama-3.1 tokenizer facts, unaudited on Qwen2.5 — `run_bandit_pv7.sh qwen25` exits 1 with that explanation rather than implying support. Adding Qwen means re-auditing both invariants and re-freezing the state bank; use pv6 for Qwen cross-model work.
    - **Frozen wording for any pv7 gate pass — do not soften:** it shows Bandit competence **under a structured, parser-assisted rationale interface with Policy-following constrained action**. It is NOT native free-generation competence: P1b's native termination still fails and the Policy that Stage 2 reads is extractor-recovered.
    - **Phase order (cheapest first, α last):** Phase 0 freeze pv6 → 1 freeze pv7 prompts → 2 build the state bank → 3 prompt-only P0/P1/P2 on frozen states → 4 Calculator (Beta-smoothed estimate) as a SEPARATE scaffold arm, never merged into a "native capability" claim → 5 α=0 ladder (N=3/T=20 → N=3/T=100 → N=20/T=100 Easy-bare **competence gate re-run**, gate rules unchanged, algorithmic baselines reusable since the environment is unchanged) → 6 the seven (rationale α, action α) cells **on frozen states** (needs `rationale_alpha`/`action_alpha`, which do not exist yet; `--steering_scope` cannot express `(−4, 0)`) → 7 full pv7 episodes, `scope=both`, only after Easy α=0 passes the gate. Mixed `−4/+4` stays deferred — it is a controller, not a unified-α claim.
    - **pv7 GATE VERDICT (2026-08-06, N=20, frozen): Easy-bare FAIL on a TIE, Hard-bare FAIL.** Easy SuffFail **0.250 vs Greedy 0.250** — rule 1 needs strict `<`, so a tie fails; rules 2–4 pass (KxMinFrac .026 vs Random .794, adherence .929, late OptFrac .731). Paired bootstrap `+0.0000 [+0.0000, +0.0000]`: model and Greedy fail **the identical five seeds (3/26/31/46/50), Jaccard 1.00, zero discordant**. Hard 0.600 vs 0.350 (rule 1 only; 6/20 never pulled the best arm). **So pv7 has NO competence anchor and every α cell is failure-mode characterization** — the words capability-effect / rescue / improvement are unavailable. Correct wording: "did not pass the gate", never "worse than Greedy".
      - **The failure mechanism is ONE-SHOT-ZERO LOCK-IN, and it is not a discovery failure.** In all five seeds the true best arm was pulled **exactly once in the opening scan, returned reward 0, and was never pulled again** (96–99 rounds later). `best_never_tried` is 0/20 — pulling once ≠ discovering. After abandonment the arm's name appears in **0/485** subsequent Policy lines; final allocation is 97/1/1/1. Policies lock via *trial count* ("EXPLOIT Button C because it has the most trials"), which is self-reinforcing. Quantified split: `uncertainty_recognition .996` vs `uncertainty_action_alignment .031` over the same 485 rounds — the model states the uncertainty and does not act on it.
      - **pv7 vs pv6 is a trade, not a regression:** pv6 passed the gate (SuffFail .150) with a noisy Stage 2 that sometimes overrode a wrong lock-in; pv7's S1 executor (`action_follows_policy` .989) faithfully executes Stage 1, so a greedy Policy becomes behaviour directly. Say "reducing execution noise exposed the Stage-1 deficit", not "pv7 is worse".
    - **FOUR interventions have now failed to move the `policy_targets_one_shot_zero` floor of ~.017, all on the same 123 frozen states (`bandit_pv7_lockin_states.json`, built by `freeze_pv7_lockin_states.py`: 20 seeds × 6 fixed rounds r4/r10/r30/r50/r75/r95 = 120 grid + 5 oracle-selected critical slots, 123 stored because 2 critical rounds coincide with r4).**
      - **Stage-1 α (`eval_pv7_stage1_alpha.py`, α∈{−4,0,+4}, action_alpha=0):** `.017/.017/.017` — **exactly constant**, only 6 of 360 cells target a low-sample arm and all sit in 2 states. But α is NOT inert: it rewrote **76–80% of rationales** while changing `policy_target` in only 4–10%. It moves the policy toward **UNTRIED (0 pulls)** arms (.041→.073 at −4) and **never** toward **ONE-SHOT-ZERO (1 pull, observed 0)** arms. Decision sharpness is the one clean dose-response: `margin` 3.42/3.95/4.23, `norm_entropy` .195/.144/.119, measured on an **unsteered Stage 2**, so it arrived through the text. Frozen claim: **α modulates policy commitment / decision sharpness; no evidence it modulates targeted information seeking.**
      - **Choice history (`eval_pv7_history_ablation.py`, H0/H1):** primary `.017→.017`; `chose_last_chosen` +0.008 with paired deltas near zero at every tail-run length (r76 shows ~60 identical letters and still does not increase persistence) — so **neither self-monitoring nor imitation was detected**. Real effect is format only: hashtags **.683→.496**, `policy_parsed` .837→.919, `action_follows_policy` .976→1.000.
      - **Beta calculator (`eval_pv7_calculator.py`, H1/C1/C2):** `.017/.025/.033` = **2/3/4 states out of 120**, all in 3 seeds, with **no signal in the 103 non-re-ranking states** (C2−C1 CI `[+0.000, +0.000]`). Meanwhile `mentions_posterior` .000/.610/**.780**, grounding error .041→**.000**, hashtags .496→.098 — the model reads and restates the posterior and still does not act. It became **more** greedy: posterior-greedy alignment .675→.715, untried targeting **.057→.008** (CI excludes 0). Verdict: the calculator mainly strengthens posterior-greedy.
      - **Frozen conclusion: the deficit is not representational.** The model can compute, read, restate and recognise uncertainty; it will not spend one choice to buy information. Next intervention class must target the uncertainty-to-action policy, not the representation.
    - **Two口径 traps that bit repeatedly in this line — always tie-tolerant, never bare `argmax`.** A bare max silently returns the first-listed tied arm. Empirical-best ties (9/123 states) inflated the history ablation's "dissociating" subset from **12 to 20**; posterior ties are **structural** (Beta(1,1) sends every `0/1` arm to exactly 1/3 and every untried arm to exactly 1/2, so 30/123 states tie) and inflated "posterior-top is a one-shot-zero arm" from **0/120 to 17/120**. `eval_pv7_calculator.py` therefore reports a **BAND** (unique-max lower bound .. tied-inclusive upper bound = 0.0%..14.2%) as the re-ranking baseline, and a C1/C2 value inside that band is consistent with arithmetic alone. Same rule as `evaluate_competence_gate.py:115`.
      - **Frozen-state scope limit (states are sampled from an α=0 trajectory and yield ONE choice with no feedback):** exploring there can never pay off, so a null means "did not change the immediate choice in already-locked states", NOT "the intervention does nothing". It is a **necessity** test, not a sufficiency test — an intervention could still work by preventing lock-in from forming, which only a full episode measures. Inference uses **paired cluster bootstrap with SEED as the unit (n=20), never state-level n=123**; a CI straddling 0 is written "未检出 / NOT DETECTED", never "no effect".
    - **pv8 = pv7 + a CHOICE HISTORY block in Stage 1** (`bandit_pv8_episode.py` + `run_bandit_pv8_episodes.py` + `run_bandit_pv8.sh` + `evaluate_competence_gate_pv8.py`). Chosen over the calculator for the full-episode α sweep because history is behaviourally near-neutral and format-positive, whereas the calculator demonstrably strengthens greedy and would confound α. **Stage 2 is byte-unchanged** (frozen S1, counts only, no history) — putting the block there too would let a repeated label prime the candidate logits directly. `bandit_pv8_episode.run_pv8_episode` reuses pv7's loop and temporarily patches `p7.build_rationale_prompt` (restored in `finally`), so steering semantics / fire counting / tapes / record schema cannot drift. **α=0 MUST be re-run** — the stored pv7 α=0 was generated without the block and is not this protocol's baseline. Cells: `pv8_easy_bare` / `_am4` / `_ap4`, `rationale_alpha ∈ {−4,0,+4}` with `action_alpha=0`, same seed bank and tapes, one directory per α (the detail JSON name carries no α). `history_block()` lives in the **generation** module and `eval_pv7_history_ablation.py` imports it — never the reverse, or an analysis edit would silently change how trajectories were generated. Resume key adds the history-block version **and a model/mask fingerprint incl. the mask's SHA256**, so a regenerated mask under the same filename is a different key. First seed of every cell is always attested (the Stage-1-has-history / Stage-2-has-none assertions only fire when an attestation exists). Prompt grows 184→323 tokens over T=100; anchor stays token 220 at every round.
    - **pv9 = pv8 + four Stage-1 modifications + a second environment. The last REWARD-MAXIMIZING protocol; SUPERSEDED by PV10 (BAI), which is itself CLOSED — but its frozen seed banks, gate rules and analysis 口径 are still what PV10 reuses.** Files: `bandit_pv9.py` (Stage-1 prompt surface, pure — no model), `bandit_pv9_episode.py`, `run_bandit_pv9_episodes.py`, `run_bandit_pv9.sh`, `evaluate_competence_gate_pv9.py`, `freeze_pv9_baseline.py`, `bandit_pv9_baseline_manifest.json`, `test_bandit_pv9.py`. Design doc: `AdaBandit.md` §1.3. Same containment pattern as pv8 — `run_pv9_episode` patches `p7.build_rationale_prompt` and restores it in `finally`, so steering semantics / fire counting / tapes / record schema cannot drift. **Stage 2 is byte-unchanged** (frozen S1, counts only): the untried cue, the score line and the history all live in Stage 1 ONLY, because putting any of them in Stage 2 would prime the candidate logits directly. **α=0 MUST be re-run** — the stored pv8 α=0 predates these prompt changes.
      - **The four Stage-1 changes**: (1) self-relevant score framing (`Your score so far: N points.`), (2) an untried-arm exploration cue, (3) generation control — `RATIONALE_MAX_TOKENS=128`, `RATIONALE_WORD_LIMIT=50`, `STOP_STRINGS=('#',)`, (4) an explicit reward-distribution statement in the header. Version constants: `STAGE1_INSTRUCTION_VERSION='p9'`, `STAGE2_INSTRUCTION_VERSION='s1'` (frozen, inherited), `SCORE_BLOCK_VERSION='score-v1'`, `UNTRIED_CUE_VERSION='cue-v1'`, `HISTORY_BLOCK_VERSION='hist-letters-v1'`, all carried in the resume key.
      - **`STOP_STRINGS` is `('#',)` and deliberately NOT `'\n\n'` — this is a measured exclusion, not a preference.** On stored pv8 data 1999/2000 rounds contain a blank line and **all 1999 fall BEFORE the Policy line** (the model writes Evidence → blank → Policy), so a `\n\n` stop string would truncate nearly every Policy. Do not "improve" the stop string without re-measuring this.
      - **Three text layers per round, and `stop_reason` is judged on the RAW text**: `rationale_raw` (untruncated), `rationale_stopped`, `rationale_clean` (what Stage 2 actually saw), with a five-value vocabulary `native_clean / stop_marker_applied / continued_after_policy / no_policy_line / empty`. An earlier build overwrote `rationale_raw` with the truncated text; the regression test now asserts a `#tag` SURVIVES in `rationale_raw` for both the steered and unsteered cells, so the old bug's signature is the failure condition.
      - **Second environment `neartie`** (K=4, probs `0.60/0.55/0.25/0.25`, T=100) with **`competence_eligible=False`**: a MECHANISM environment, never a competence anchor. Its gate output is a readout against its own frozen Greedy baseline and must never be reported as a competence verdict. It exists because Easy is ceiling-adjacent and leaves α no headroom (same baseline-ceiling problem as pv6 Easy and Qwen's MMLU betting cell). **Easy and neartie SHARE one seed bank** — `build_seed_bank` depends only on seed and k and both are K=4 — which is what makes the two environments seed-paired; `run_bandit_pv9.sh` derives `FORMAL_SEEDS` from `easy` and passes it to the neartie cells on that basis.
      - **PV9 has its OWN frozen manifest** (`bandit_pv9_baseline_manifest.json`), so `bandit_pv6_baseline_manifest.json` stays byte-unchanged and every stored pv6/pv7/pv8 citation still verifies. `freeze_pv9_baseline.py --check` additionally asserts PV9-Easy's basis is IDENTICAL to pv6's — a silent divergence there would mean PV9-Easy and pv8-Easy are judged against different bases. **Its float comparison carries a 1e-9 RELATIVE tolerance** (non-floats, seed banks, structure and types still compare exactly): Easy ran on python 3.10/arm64 and later cells on 3.12/x86_64, where identical code differs in the last ULP (`0.04` vs `0.04000000000000001`). The gate compares 0.05-scale point estimates, so 1e-9 cannot move a verdict, while a real basis change is orders of magnitude larger and still fails. A MISMATCH now prints WHICH paths differ.
      - **`run_bandit_pv9.sh {llama3} [SMOKE|SMOKE_AM4|A0_EASY|AM4_EASY|AP4_EASY|A0_NT|AM4_NT|AP4_NT|GATE|ALL]`.** `ALL` runs the **six formal cells only** and skips every `SMOKE*` step. `SMOKE_AM4` exists because α=0 registers no hook anywhere, so the α=0 smoke never touches `regenerate`, the mask file, or the fire counter — without it the steered path would first execute in a formal cell. The manifest `--check` runs BEFORE any GPU time is spent.
    - **PV9 GATE VERDICT (Easy-bare, N=20): PASS on all four rules — the FIRST pass in the pv7 lineage** (pv7 failed on a tie, SuffFail .250 vs Greedy .250). SuffFail **0.200** vs Greedy 0.250; KxMinFrac .038 (T) / .076 (T/2) vs Random .794 and decreasing; post-discovery adherence .887; late OptFrac .796; arms_discovered 3.95/4; churn .032. The four Stage-1 changes dissolved pv7's one-shot-zero lock-in. **Frozen wording still applies and must not be softened**: competence under a STRUCTURED, PARSER-ASSISTED, CUE-SCAFFOLDED interface with Policy-following constrained action — NOT native free generation. 78% of rationales stop only via the `#` marker (`native_clean` ≈9%), so "parser-assisted interface succeeded, native stopping failed" is still the correct description.
    - **PV9 Easy α RESULT: outcome-layer NULL, mechanism-layer DOSE-RESPONSE.** Nothing significant on late_opt_frac / opt_frac / regret / suffix_failure / k_min_frac / greedy_frac (paired Wilcoxon, n=20). But EXPLORE stance **.080/.049/.043** (α=−4/0/+4; p=.015 / .027), explore keywords .095/.056/.052 (p=.011), and Stage-2 margin 4.18/4.27/**4.70** (p=.019 at +4) with norm_entropy .124/.119/.089. **Stage 2 is completely unsteered** (`action: 0`, verified 20/20), so the sharpening arrives THROUGH THE TEXT — a full-episode replication of `eval_pv7_stage1_alpha`'s unsteered-Stage-2 dose-response (margin 3.42/3.95/4.23). Discovery is at ceiling and immovable: `best_never_tried` 0/20 in every cell, `novel_switch_rate` .030 identical to three decimals across all α, first best pull ≈ round 1.7. **So α moves the policy's WORDING and COMMITMENT, not information seeking** — the full-episode form of pv7's `uncertainty_recognition .996` vs `uncertainty_action_alignment .031`. Format is untouched at ±4 (`no_policy_line` / `empty_rationale` / anchor-collision / redundant-evidence all 0 across 6000 rounds; length and hashtag rate flat), so unlike CGT +8 or Qwen betting +8 there is no over-steer artifact. **Half of this null is the Easy ceiling** — NearTie is the control that separates "α does nothing" from "the environment left α nothing to do".
      - **Do NOT judge whether steering fired from a single seed.** At α=−4, seed 0 round 0 is byte-identical to α=0 despite `steering_fires: 900`. Across matched state (rounds sharing an identical `(choices, feedbacks)` prefix) α rewrites **69% (−4) / 59% (+4)** of rounds. Same matched-state 口径 as `analyze_bandit_pv6_alpha.py`; same-round-index would understate it.
      - **`policy_target_source` is `policy_first_clause` in 6000/6000 rounds** (there is no `policy_segment` value — that name is wrong). Zero fallback parses, so every reported target came from the strict first-decision-clause extractor.
      - **Cross-host caveat, load-bearing for the remaining cells.** PV9's α contrast is **paired by seed**, so an α cell run on a different host than its α=0 baseline mixes the machine difference into the α effect — bf16 greedy is not byte-reproducible across GPUs (see the GSM8K cross-machine rule; one re-run gave 205/300 text mismatches). Easy's three cells ran on 182. A NearTie cell on a different host is a PREVIEW, not paired evidence; keep all three cells of an environment on one host.
      - **PV9 NEARTIE RESULT (2026-08-11, N=20, all three cells complete and attested): the Easy null is NOT a ceiling artifact — α's effect profile is IDENTICAL in an environment that has headroom.** NearTie removes the ceiling (late_opt_frac .556 vs Easy's .796; `P(empirical-best set contains the TRUE best | t>=50)` is only **.468**, so greedy is no longer near-optimal), yet: uncertainty targeting stays at the floor (**unique-max 0/164, 0/246, 0/236 — exactly zero in all three cells**, tie-inclusive .0032/.0037/.0018), every outcome is null (late_opt .401/.556/.538, suffix_failure .500/.350/.350, final score 46.2/46.9/48.0), switching/churn null, and the only surviving effects are again Stage-1 wording (EXPLORE stance .097/.065/.051, p=.012/.012). **Five pre-registered primary readouts, Holm across one 16-test family → survivors NONE** (min adj p = .193). DiD (NearTie−Easy) CIs all contain 0 → environment interaction **NOT DETECTED**. Full tables: `AdaBandit.md` §4.
      - **Two 口径 traps this analysis established, both of which produced a wrong reading first:** (a) **`σ` and `n` are NOT separable in PV9** — corr(posterior SD, log n) = **−.994**, and residualizing SD on `1/√n` leaves 22% of its variation, so the choice-model interaction is named **`b_information`** (weighting of low-sample/high-uncertainty options) and **must never be called β or presented as a GP-UCB uncertainty bonus**; the primary model uses ONE pre-specified parameterization (`1/√n`) with posterior SD as a sensitivity check, never both. (b) **the non-greedy decomposition's apparent α gradient is a lock-wrong-seed artifact** — Easy `costly_error` reads .517/.148/.041 pooled but collapses to .073/.049/.034 once each cell's own `late_opt_frac<.2` episodes are excluded, and the per-seed median is 0 in all three cells (p=.317); a single failing seed contributes ~90 rounds. `analyze_bandit_pv9.py` prints that robustness row automatically.
      - **`stance_behavior_alignment_broad` must be read with its ABSOLUTE numerator, not its rate.** EXPLORE-stance counts fall with α (Easy 160/98/86) while the broad "non-greedy ∪ untried" numerator is ~constant (95/81/82), and **almost all of that numerator is cue-scaffolded untried coverage (77/79/79), with true uncertainty-max at 5/2/1**. So α changes how often the EXPLORE *label* is emitted and how often it goes unfulfilled (falling back to empirical-greedy: 65/17/4), NOT the amount of information seeking. Do not quote the rate alone.
      - **`uncertainty_calibration` is ANTI-calibrated, and it is a protocol/model property rather than an α effect** (descriptive): ρ(hedge, pull count) = **+.178 / +.163** at α=0/+4 on Easy (p<1e−12) and +.189/+.159 on NearTie — hedging RISES with sample size, hedge@n>50 ≈ .97. α=−4 is the one cell where it vanishes (Easy ρ=−.002). Meanwhile **every strictly-parseable rate claim is correct (1.0000 in all six cells)**: the model reads its own evidence perfectly and still reasons about uncertainty backwards. Word it as "all STRICTLY PARSEABLE rate claims are correct", never "arithmetic is fully correct" — a permissive regex reads ~.80 only because "79 trials of Button C … 21 trials of OTHER buttons" binds 21 to C.
    - **PV10 = the last BANDIT MAIN LINE (2026-08-14), now CLOSED. A reframe, not an increment: self-paced Best-Arm Identification / pure exploration, not reward maximization.** Only the FINAL recommended arm is scored (identification accuracy / simple regret); cumulative regret is not the objective. Files: `bandit_pv10.py` (prompt/orders/parser, pure — no model), `bandit_pv10_episode.py`, `run_bandit_pv10_episodes.py`, `run_bandit_pv10.sh`, `evaluate_pv10_capability.py` + `pv10_capability_manifest.json`, `pv10_env_prescreen.py` + `pv10_prescreen_manifest.json`, `analyze_bandit_pv10.py`, `test_bandit_pv10*.py`. Design doc: `AdaBandit.md` §4 (§4.3 = the protocol frozen BEFORE α=0 ran).
      - **SINGLE-STAGE (PV10-B): one generation per round emits `Reason: …` then `Policy: SAMPLE Button X` or `Policy: COMMIT Button X`. There is no Stage 2.** Justified by PV9's Stage1→Stage2 consistency of **`.994–1.000`** (near-saturated, NOT strictly identical) — Stage 2 had degenerated to an identity map, and SAMPLE/COMMIT is 2-dimensional so it does not fit Stage 2's candidate-scoring interface anyway. **BINDING CONSEQUENCE: PV10 measures a `joint reasoning–decision effect`.** PV9's argument — "α's sharpening passes through the TEXT into a verified-unsteered executor" — is NOT available here and must never be reused; it was only possible because Stage 2 was separately attested as unsteered.
      - **`Reason: ` ends on token 220** (measured on the real Llama-3.1 tokenizer, not inherited from PV9's `Evidence: `); candidates are bare letters. **LLAMA3-ONLY** for the same reason as pv7.
      - **`expected_fires = model_calls × n_steered_layers × tail_tokens`, and `model_calls = τ − k·FORCED_INIT_PER_ARM + 1` = τ−3 at K=4.** A full-budget episode is **873 fires, NOT PV9's 900** — the forced init consumes samples without generating. L=9 for the `11-20` band (`decoder_layer_range` is half-open). Attested per episode; a mismatch raises.
      - **Termination is three mutually exclusive values**: `autonomous_commit` / `forced_commit` / `invalid_policy`. **An invalid policy TERMINATES the episode** — `tau` is null (no fabricated stopping time) and the exit time is recorded separately as `n_at_invalid`. In stopping analyses invalid is a **competing exit**, never deleted from the risk set.
      - **Two-tier failure separation**: a model that generates unparseable content is `invalid_policy` (behaviour); an exception / empty return / stale wrapper is `EpisodeInfrastructureError` (fail closed, re-run via resume). A `None` fire count is a FAILURE, not a skip — it means a stale server `llms.py`.
      - **Orders belong to the CELL, not the seed.** `assign_orders` counterbalances display order × initial-pull order across the WHOLE 20-seed bank (exact 5/5/5/5); passing a SUBSET re-derives different orders and silently breaks pairing, so the driver REFUSES anything but the full bank. `make_orders` is per-seed and marked NOT FOR FORMAL RUNS. Extension requires a manifest leaving the original 20 untouched.
      - **Environment `(0.60, 0.50, 0.40, 0.30)`, T=100, K=4, frozen after an offline prescreen** (`pv10_env_prescreen.py`): Uniform .7205 / interface-adapted Sequential Halving .7428 / TTTS .7620 / Greedy .5125 (BELOW Uniform — Greedy is a failure-mode comparator, **never an upper bound** in BAI); LUCB was 100% censored at T=100. Pre-registered criterion **P1 passed, P2 FAILED**, and the environment was frozen anyway rather than retuned — P1/P2 were DEMOTED to diagnostics because algorithmic baselines are not central to PV10's question. Two caveats this leaves binding: TTTS's near-uniform allocation is a **MARGINAL** distribution (it still chooses conditionally each step), so flat model allocation is NOT evidence of absent directed exploration; and LUCB censoring means no episode earned a stopping certificate under THAT bound — the rule is **sufficient and possibly conservative, not necessary**, so it does NOT follow that model commits are evidence-poor.
      - **Minimum capability check (C1–C4, frozen BEFORE the driver was written) excludes only a fully inexecutable interface** — no baseline comparison, no accuracy floor, no stopping requirement. It is defined on the **α=0 cell**; a ±4 PASS is a descriptive interface check, never a gate result. **C4 ≠ "evidence utilisation"**: it says the model commits inside its OWN empirical-best set, whose random reference is not strictly 1/K because ties enlarge the set, and which the model's own concentrated sampling produced.
      - **PV10-B α=0/±4 RESULT (N=20 each, all cells attested): capability PASS, but identification is at the FLOOR and no reliable α effect.** acc .263/.278/.100 (α=−4/0/+4; paired McNemar α=0 vs +4 **p=.453** — the +4 drop is directional only). τ median 8/13/11.5 → the model spends ~10% of its 100-sample budget. Stopping time shows no detectable α effect (paired Wilcoxon p=.075–.71, and the pattern is U-shaped, not monotone). **The low accuracy is NOT merely early stopping**: against a **matched-budget Uniform** computed at each episode's own τ (the T=100 figure of .72 is not a legal comparator) the model is still **.16–.35 BELOW** Uniform. Mechanism: sampling is concentrated (max-arm share median ~.63, `min_trials` median **1**, 16/18 episodes leave some arm at a single pull), so at commit the TRUE best is in the empirical-best set only **.556** of the time — that is the ceiling, and the model reads it faithfully (.778) to land at .278. The text layer shows PV9's split again: **5/18 commits explicitly say the evidence is insufficient and commit anyway**.
      - **口径 trap that produced a WRONG reading first: a POOLED per-action rate is weighted by episode length.** Pooled leader-sampling looked like a large α effect (+4 .477 vs α=0 .763) but **vanishes when paired by seed** (median Δ=0.000, p=1.00) — 5 long episodes contributed 75% of +4's pooled SAMPLE actions. Pair by seed; the unit of inference is the seed (n=20).
      - **`analyze_bandit_pv10.py` is the frozen analyzer.** Three 口径 pinned there because they silently diverge: **top-two share is always a PAIR** — `true_top2_share` (ground truth, DIAGNOSTIC only) and `empirical_top2_share` (lock-in sensitive, never alone as exploration quality); on PV10-B they point OPPOSITE ways (.49 vs .81 at α=0, against a .50 uniform reference) = concentrated on the pair it wrongly believes is best. **Allocation entropy is normalised by log(K)** → [0,1]. **Final accuracy scores the single submitted arm; ties are NOT credited**, while empirical-best sets stay tie-tolerant (matching `evaluate_competence_gate.py:115`).
      - **PV10-A = the fixed-budget MECHANISM CONTROL** (`bandit_pv10a.py`, `run_bandit_pv10a_episodes.py`, `run_bandit_pv10a.sh`, `test_bandit_pv10a.py`): SAMPLE only until the budget is spent, then PV10-B's **byte-identical** terminal prompt asks for the commit. Everything else — environment, seed bank, tapes, order bank, parser, anchor, steering — is the SAME object; `run_pv10_episode` gained a `prompt_module` parameter **defaulting to `bandit_pv10`**, verified byte-inert for PV10-B by running an identical FakeVC episode under the frozen and current modules. It separates a too-low commitment threshold from a broken acquisition policy. **Interpretation limit: PV10-A removes the COMMIT option from round 1, so it is NOT "the same task with more budget"** — an improvement reads as *forced continuation / removal of the stopping option improved acquisition*. The A-vs-B prefix comparison is a **paired-seed prefix comparison** (descriptive): states diverge at the first differing choice, so it does not identify the causal effect of extra budget.
      - **PV10-A v1 (`pv10a_a0/_am4/_ap4`) is VOID — interface failure, not behaviour (2026-08-17).** 58/60 episodes ended `invalid_policy` and **0/20 α=0 episodes reached the fixed budget `n=100`**, so the fixed-budget control never exercised its own design. Two independent root causes, both fixed in **`pv10-strict-v2`**; full record in `pv10_capability_amendment_02.json`.
        - **RC1 — stop parity.** `llms.VicundaModel.generate()` had **no `stop_strings` parameter** while `regenerate()` did. α=0 registers no hook and therefore takes the `generate` path, so **α=0 cells ran with NO stop marker while their own ±4 cells stopped on `#`** — the two arms of one experiment had different generation boundaries, a confound rather than a behaviour. Evidence: PV10-A median generation 489 chars at α=0 vs 226 at +4. `generate()` now takes `stop_strings` (default `None`, so every pre-existing caller is byte-identical) and forwards the tokenizer as HF requires; the episode runner passes `p10.STOP_STRINGS` on **both** paths. **Any new two-path experiment must assert stop parity** — `test_pv10_stop_parity.py` does this by source inspection.
        - **RC2 — runtime/parser contract.** HF `stop_strings` halts only **after** emitting the marker, so `#` stayed in the returned text while `_POLICY_RE` (anchored on `$`, tolerating only `[.!]`) read the line as `malformed`. 47/58 terminating rounds died here: **33 bare trailing `#`** and 14 `# Note: …`. `bandit_pv10.apply_stop_boundary()` now truncates at the first marker **excluding it**, and only the truncated text is parsed. **This is a control-token boundary fix, NOT parser leniency** — `_POLICY_RE` is byte-unchanged and prose-after-arm / conflicting-directives / no-directive / arm-out-of-set / SAMPLE-at-terminal all remain invalid (asserted).
        - **Three text layers per round**: `raw_generation` (untruncated audit) / `generation_stopped` (what was parsed) / `stop_marker_truncated`.
        - **A falsified diagnosis worth not repeating:** the first proposed fix was deleting PV10-A's "The Policy line must name exactly one button." clause as an echoed meta-instruction. **PV10-B contains the identical clause and does not fail**, and the 33-case majority has no echoed text to delete — the clause was never the cause. Check whether a suspect clause is shared with a passing protocol before editing it.
        - **Offline re-parse is DIAGNOSTIC ONLY.** Under v2, PV10-B v1 changes 1 round in 412 (α=0), 1 in 307 (−4), 0 in 343 (+4) — so most *actions* are unchanged, but a changed action alters every subsequent state and **re-parsing cannot regenerate the trajectory**. PV10-B v1 is RETAINED and citable against the v1 basis (`pv10_capability_manifest.frozen_pv10-strict-v1.json`); a strict A/B comparison requires re-running PV10-B under v2.
        - **`POLICY_PARSER_VERSION` v1→v2 propagates into `interface_tag`**, so v1 cannot resume into v2; v2 results go to `pv10a_v2_*`. The manifest guard fired correctly on `module_sha256` + `versions` and was not bypassed — the v1 basis was preserved before the deliberate re-freeze, and `criteria` / `seed_bank` / `order_bank` / `FROZEN_CRITERIA` (`be205de8a77bd30e`) are all unchanged.
        - **Two-GPU layout**: `llms.py` loads with `device_map="auto"`, which claims every **visible** device, so `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` is what actually separates concurrent cells — without it two jobs map across both cards and collide. `run_bandit_pv10a.sh llama3 PM4` runs −4 then +4 **sequentially on one card** while `A0` runs on the other. **Concurrency does not change the reading order**: α=0 is what says whether the control works at all, and if it does not reach `n=100` the ±4 cells are void for the same reason v1 was.
      - **PV10-A v2 VERDICT (frozen 2026-08-17): `interface-compromised diagnostic`, and PV10-A is CLOSED — no v3.** Full tables in `AdaBandit.md` §4.4. Attestation is clean (873 fires at ±4, 0 at α=0, identical `interface_tag` across cells), but 30–40% of episodes still exit `invalid_policy` mid-episode (−4: 6/20, α=0: 7/20, +4: 8/20). Per-round validity is ~99.4–99.6%; ~97 rounds of free generation compound it into episode failure. **The finding that closes PV10-A: forced continuation did NOT produce exploration.** Surviving episodes do spend the full budget (`n_samples` median 100 in all three cells) and still land at `min_trials=1` and `max_arm_share≈.6`, with α=0 `true_top2=.408` against a rate-based `emp_top2=.734` — near-total concentration on a self-selected pair that is usually the wrong pair. **So PV10-B's bottleneck is the ACQUISITION policy, not a too-low commitment threshold**, and a v3 that merely truncates generation more cleanly would buy interface hygiene without touching the mechanism.
        - **Cross-α accuracy is UNINTERPRETABLE here, and the reason is structural: each cell drops a DIFFERENT seed set** (α=0 drops {4,19,31}, +4 drops {5,26,46,53}), so the three-cell table compares three different subsets of the environment, and the dropout is itself α-dependent. The apparent `.154 → .583` spread is mostly denominator. Paired: α=0 vs +4 **n=9, `.222 vs .444`, discordant 2/0, McNemar p=.50**; all three complete on only n=7 (`2/7, 2/7, 3/7`). ITT over 20 seeds is `.250/.100/.350`. **No reliable α effect at any 口径.** Always attach the denominator (α=0 completed-subset accuracy is `2/13`, never a bare `.154`).
        - **The two invalid shapes load differently by α** (−4: 5 conflicting / 1 malformed; +4: 6 malformed / 2 conflicting), mirroring the CGT-seq / HaluEval **散 vs 垮** asymmetry — so accumulated format failure is partly an α-modulated behaviour, not purely interface noise. It does not rescue the cells; it belongs in the diagnostic record.

        - **`empirical_top2_share` MUST be rate-based and MUST be reported beside `true_top2_share`.** Definition: read `rounds[*].pre_counts` (`[successes, trials]`), compute each arm's empirical success rate before every adaptive SAMPLE, take the two highest-rate arms, ask whether the sample landed in that set; denominator = adaptive SAMPLE actions only, excluding the 4 forced-init pulls. PV10-A α=0 = **900/1248 = .721 (strict) / 916/1248 = .734 (tie-tolerant, the house 口径 per `evaluate_competence_gate.py:115`)**, insensitive to whether invalids or the terminal round are filtered. **A count-based top-2 share is BANNED** (ranking arms by trial count then summing those counts floors at .5 for K=4 and merely restates `max_arm_share`). **`history` stores arm labels only**, so recomputing from `history` + `final_counts` silently degenerates to the banned form — it must come from `rounds`.

The one positive, `osz_revisit_frac` .212→.354, is an artifact of τ rising 12→100 (~8× more sampling opportunities), not directed acquisition.

B's longest same-arm run (median 4.0 vs C's 66.5) is short only because B's τ is 12. The cue lengthened the episode and made the existing pathology visible. Do not attribute it to C.
          - **SAMPLE and COMMIT follow different local heuristics.** SAMPLE repeats the max-count incumbent, but PV10-B sometimes COMMITs to a low-count empirical-rate maximum: committed-arm trials median **4**, and **8/19** commits select an arm with only 1–2 trials; all 8 are in the tie-tolerant empirical-rate-max set. This is a behavioral `sampling–commit asymmetry`, not one coherent greedy policy. C changes the stopping regime, so its COMMIT distribution is not a homogeneous replication of B's.

          - **`emp_top2` is an action-vs-rate-ordering separation metric, NOT evidence quality.** B's .837 reflects stopping early (the incumbent is still in the empirical top two); C runs 100 rounds, pins one arm at 97 pulls, and keeps sampling an incumbent whose rate has fallen while one-shot `1/1` arms hold the top rates. Never read a higher `emp_top2` as better evidence use.
